Decision log numbered (TL2 ratifies):

- DL-34 ADOPT the vendor-neutral ProviderAdapter contract (extensions/flauz-models/src/contract/) as the stable internal port for every future provider adapter (ports + HTTP shapes, zero vendor SDKs).
- DL-35 ADOPT the 14-code provider error taxonomy with its fixed retryable/terminal table as the shared retry seam with the orchestration runtime (runtime consumes retryClass/retryAfterMs).
- DL-36 ADOPT the durable .flauz/models state family (providers/capabilities/routing-policy envelopes + routing-decisions JSONL ledger + tool-policy file) as the model-fabric persistence contract.
- DL-37 ADOPT the zero-network default posture: remote vendors ship disabled without credentials; the default routing rule targets flauz-mock, explicitly labeled; enabling a real provider is an explicit workspace act recorded in providers.json.
- DL-38 ADOPT the deterministic truncation-priority policy (tool results -> attachments -> tail-keep truncation -> conversation drops, system/pinned/last-turn protected) as the context-budget compiler default.
- DL-39 ADOPT policy-records-only MCP/tool posture (no re-gating of the native lm/MCP UX; agent-scoped tool-set policy consumed at assembly time).
- DL-40 PROPOSE live-provider verification drills as a network-bearing follow-up work item (fixture evidence is not live evidence) — scheduled with the S-lanes.
- DL-41 PROPOSE routing-decision recording from the agent bridge once orchestration consumes the router — scheduled with TL2-001 M4/M5 continuation.
- DL-42 ADOPT the memory-substrate law: journal rewrites are sanctioned only when recorded (compaction/promote/demote carry audit rows: dropped ids, source+result ids).
- DL-43 ADOPT memory record ids minted-from-max-seq (not positional); uniqueness + id/journal scope law governs.
- DL-44 ADOPT authorization-bearing memory never auto-promotes (humanApproved + actor human required; compaction never evicts) — promoted from lane rule to program rule (mirrors the human-gate posture).
- DL-45 ADOPT the private-context boundary living in the pure retrieval function (enforced where every consumer must pass through) as the pattern for future boundary laws.
- DL-46 ADOPT workflow recovery semantics: at-least-once per interrupted step (append-only ledger keeps interrupted-attempt evidence rows; coherent state, not exactly-once effects).
- DL-47 ADOPT version-pinned recovery (a run recovers only against the spec version it started with; a bumped spec requires a fresh run) — extended to the durable graph.
- DL-48 ADOPT 'reported-not-verified' as a first-class result state (unverified A2A results labeled with their specific reason, never passed off as verified).
- DL-49 ADOPT the memory index shape: derived entries + explicit journal registry appended BEFORE the journal append (crash-safe ordering; entries rebuildable) for any future no-readdir index.

Worker A's number-free proposals arrive with its M4/M5 REPORT (in flight); they will be numbered DL-50+.

- DL-52 ADOPT the boundary event stream (connected/disconnected/event-gap/reconnected/recovered/health-degraded/shutdown) as the supervision vocabulary for TL4 connectivity/degradation rows.
- DL-53 ADOPT the outcome-unknown discipline as a program rule for every seam consumer: a lost response to a side-effecting method is never retried optimistically; reconciliation = explicit state re-read + a NEW attempt key.
- DL-54 PROPOSE the additive seam extensions from the usage map (flauz.orch.* journal/approval/lease/recovery/verify surfaces, flauz.events.since replay) as TL1-003 follow-ups co-designed with TL2 — each lands via the sanctioned extension path only.

TL2 Worker A2 proposals (PR #26, TL2 ratifies):
- DL-57 ADOPT the orchestration protocol contract (flauz.orch/v1 + versioned JSON schema + typed mirror) as the authoritative message-shape contract between the extension layer and the future stateful orchestration service, composed ABOVE the TL1-003 seam.
- DL-58 ADOPT the orch-only mismatch rule: a domain-layer version mismatch never kills the seam session; transport and domain failures degrade at different layers.
- DL-59 ADOPT the closed-set typed failure taxonomy (flauz.orch.err.*) with fail-closed mapping (unknown codes -> internal, provenance preserved).

- DL-61 ADOPT approval-expiry semantics: service-only, deadline-bearing requests, fail-closed cancellation (never auto-granted), expiry from op + drive loop + recovery pass; deadline-less requests hold until a human decides.
- DL-62 ADOPT the gate-terminal policy rule: cancelled steps with retryPlanned-false failure records count as permanently failed; user/dependency cancellations stay out.
- DL-63 ADOPT the evidence-id notation normalization (E-NNNNNN discipline derived from the ledger seq; the seam's unpadded string is a projection detail).

- DL-65 PROPOSE routing-decision ledger mirroring (extends DL-41: route-decided rows carry the evidence-bearing pattern).
- DL-66 PROPOSE the graph-level approval expiry question (graph submissions never time out; a human must eventually decide) — deliberately v0-undefined, consistent with the 'manual' default policy.
- DL-67 ADOPT the approval-interruption kill-recover coverage (approval requested -> process death -> recovery -> approval STILL gates execution) as part of the extended kill matrix.

Owner: TL2 / helper from TL4 Worker B

Scope: independent runtime battery for restart, cancellation, provider failure, approval interruption, lease conflicts, multi-agent collaboration and evidence/provenance recovery.

### Surge coordination law

- TL2 owns Agent OS semantics and accepts the final integration.
- Seconded workers may implement additive adapter/test work but may not redefine another TL's contracts.
- Shared-file collisions are resolved by contract extraction or additive adapters first; no broad refactor to make ownership convenient.
- A seconded worker is released back to its home TL when its bounded surge work-order is complete.
- No CopilotKit/OpenMuse dependency is introduced by the surge.

The contract at THREE levels: (1) flauz-workflow envelope — pre-dispatch cancellation checkpoint in WorkflowService.run's tool loop (task state re-read before every tool call; a terminal-cancelled task never starts the next step; ONE cancel-attributed ledger note row + observational cancel-attribution event carrying the cancel's actor; typed WorkflowRunCancelledError); (2) flauz-workspace TaskService — typed StaleRunCancelledError from appendEvent for transitions into a terminal-cancelled task (the stale run unwinds cleanly; every other illegal transition keeps the generic state-machine error); (3) flauz-agent/core runtime — driveGraph level-1 downstream gate (pre-dispatch state re-read; cancel-attributed cancel-observed journal row via the DL-60 evidence-bearing path; typed DriveReport.cancelled/cancelObservation outcome) + level-2 in-flight abort (post-effect state re-read; a mid-flight-cancelled step is never completed); orchProtocol maps 'stale-run-cancelled' onto the existing illegal-transition wire failure (closed taxonomy preserved). ## Cross-TL rule

No TL may wait for another TL to begin useful work.

When an interface is not implemented:
- define the contract;
- create a local fixture/mock;
- continue independently;
- integrate when the real implementation becomes available.

A TL may depend on another TL for final integration, but never for starting work.

A finding must include evidence level, reproduction, owning TL, acceptance test and architecture impact before implementation begins.

#### TL2 decision record — DL-78 (P2-FIX-104, decision-first per the master handoff chain) (2026-09-30)

P2-FIX-104's finding text proposes two semantics ("either `releaseLease` mirrors the release onto the bus … or the composed claim path (`claimStepLease`) treats a store-side free lease as authoritative over a stale bus notice") and marks the change "decision first, then implementation". The survey of the standing decision log (DL-50..DL-77 — the seam/lease/execution laws, including DL-60 evidence-bearing transitions, DL-66 the deliberately-v0-undefined expiry question, DL-72 lease-reuse, DL-77 serialized appends, plus the landed lease contract's acquire-path laws) confirms NO standing law settles the store-vs-bus precedence on RELEASE — a genuine contract decision was required. TL2 (the A2A/orchestration lease-semantics owner) decides and records it here, BEFORE the worker implementation dispatch:

- **DL-78 ADOPT the release-mirror law:** a durable lease release (`lease-released` journal row) PROJECTS onto the A2A bus — the release mints a bus release/retraction notice, `bus.activeClaimOf(<resource>)` returns `undefined` once the release lands, and a subsequent `claimStepLease` by any claimant proceeds with both layers in agreement (exactly one acquisition row; the bus projection names the new holder — the finding's acceptance test verbatim).

Architectural basis (why the mirror, not the claim-path precedence override):
1. **Projection fidelity:** the v0 doctrine names the bus notice the "watcher mirror" of the durable store's journal; DL-60/DL-77 make that journal the authoritative, evidence-bearing, transition-lock-serialized record. A mirror that outlives its source's release INVERTS the authority — the projection then refuses what the source has already freed. The precedence override (option b) would leave that inversion standing for every non-claim reader (watchers, `activeClaimOf` consumers) while patching only the claim path.
2. **The lease contract's own tolerance lineage:** the landed acquire-path contract already holds that EXPIRED leases don't conflict — a RELEASED lease is a strictly stronger case (an explicit, journaled, claimant-initiated transition); a bus that refuses a claim on a released lease contradicts its own expired-lease tolerance.
3. **The no-orphan-state criterion:** the acceptance journey routed this finding precisely because divergent holder views are orphan state; only the mirror semantics eliminates the divergence at its source rather than masking it at one consumer.

Scope guards (what DL-78 does NOT change): the `leaseUntil` horizon stays advisory for EXPIRY semantics (an un-released lease that expires is unchanged — the documented v0 posture, kin to DL-66's deliberately-undefined questions); fail-closed release is preserved (a non-holder release of a LIVE lease is still refused — no silent takeover); the store stays authoritative (the mirror is a projection retraction, not a second enforcement source); no state-machine change (the finding's "small" architecture-impact class).

Implementation routing: the P2-FIX-104 worker order's §7 station note is pinned to this law (the release mirrors onto the bus; additive release/retraction notice + projection retraction); the worker implements on branch `flauz-p2fix/p2-fix-104` at pinned base `e063c12b951` per the claim record; TL4 independent retest closes the finding after landing. Dispatch state at decision time: the earlier 104 session (send-during-crunch wedge) was voided; the fresh dispatch carries the pinned §7; 103 re-dispatched fresh (its earlier zombie voided — prompt accepted 17:17Z, assistant slot empty 2.6h, zero progress); 101 dispatches as a slot frees.

#### TL3 decision record — DL-79 (P2-FIX-106, decision-first per the routing record) (2026-10-01)

P2-FIX-106's finding consolidates the three pinned real-Chromium divergences (F-DELIVERY, F-POPUP-URL, F-OPENER-BLOCK) into one redesign decision the standing INTEGRATION-GAP record reserves for TL adjudication. The survey of the standing decision log confirms NO standing law settles the gate's PLACEMENT or its URL-observation strategy — the landed TL3-002 semantics pin page-session placement with attach-time URL reading, and real Chromium honors neither. TL3 (the browser security-gate semantics owner) decides and records it here, BEFORE the worker implementation dispatch:

- **DL-79 ADOPT the browser-level placement + Fetch-domain pre-use observation law:** the popup/new-target gate moves to BROWSER-level auto-attach (`Target.setAutoAttach` with `waitForDebuggerOnStart` at the browser scope — the only placement real Chromium delivers `window.open` targets to), and the pending destination is observed PRE-USE through the Fetch domain on the held target: `Fetch.enable` with request interception, the first `Fetch.requestPaused` carries the request URL (the gate's URL input — `targetInfo.url` being EMPTY at attach is accepted as a real-Chromium fact, not worked around), the policy verdict is computed with the OPENER session's initiator class. **Deny path:** `Fetch.failRequest` (the request is aborted BEFORE the wire — zero committed loads, zero bytes to the denied host) + `Target.closeTarget` (the held target is destroyed before use) + the popup-gate DENY event. **Allow path:** `Fetch.disable` + `Runtime.runIfWaitingForDebugger` release (the F-RELEASE-CMD law) + the popup-gate ALLOW event, after which the target attaches as a session tab per the landed contract. **No-URL popups** (`window.open()` with no destination — no request ever pauses): release immediately on opener attribution (`targetInfo.openerId` names the opener; a non-session opener's popup is NOT the runtime's to gate).

Architectural basis (why browser-level + Fetch, not the alternatives):
1. **The pre-use invariant:** the landed gate's own contract promises the deny path sends ZERO wire commands to denied hosts (the J3 fail-closed row class). The post-commit observe-and-close alternative (hold → resume → read the committed URL → close) would let denied content LOAD before the verdict — an observable weakening of a pinned security posture. Fetch interception preserves pre-use semantics on real Chromium: the request pauses before the wire, the abort precedes any load.
2. **The delivery fact:** F-DELIVERY is not a bug to patch but a placement constraint — page-session auto-attach demonstrably receives nothing for browser-level popups. Any correct gate must listen at the browser scope; the only question was the URL source, which Fetch answers at the network layer where the destination is knowable before use.
3. **The opener-block timing shape:** F-OPENER-BLOCK makes gate DECISIVENESS a requirement, not a nicety — a held popup blocks the opener's JS, so the gate must reach a verdict on the FIRST paused request and act (deny-close or allow-release) immediately; no indefinite hold is lawful. The no-request window (about:blank popups) must not hold at all — hence the opener-attribution release rule.
4. **Evidence fidelity:** `openerId` at attach is the only reliable provenance for a held target (whose page opened it) and is therefore carried on the gate event — the audit surface keeps attribution even though the URL arrives later via Fetch.

Scope guards (what DL-79 does NOT change): page-session auto-attach stays for tab-scoped session work (unchanged); the policy engine, trust postures and initiator classes are untouched (the gate CONSULTS them, at a different placement); session identity/provenance unchanged; the journal contract takes only ADDITIVE fields (P2-FIX-105 `navigated` events unchanged); the `PopupGateEvent` contract gains `observedUrl` + `openerId` (additive; the legacy `url` field becomes the observed URL, empty-at-attach documented); evidence timing moves from attach-time to first-request-time — the unit-pinned TL3-002 gate tests are RE-PINNED to the new contract in the same change (the sanctioned semantics change; the fake CDP transport re-models real browser-level delivery so unit tests keep pinning the wire, not the fake).

Implementation routing: the P2-FIX-106 worker order pins this law (§7 station note); the worker implements on branch `flauz-p2fix/p2-fix-106` at the claim record's pinned base; the real-Chromium drill's F-DELIVERY/F-POPUP-URL/F-OPENER-BLOCK pins flip from FINDING-pinned to assertion-pinned; TL4 independent retest closes the finding after landing.

#### TL3 decision record — DL-80 (P2-FIX-107, decision-first per the routing record) (2026-10-01)

P2-FIX-107's finding asks where URL redaction belongs: at-record (journal/popup-gate records normalize at write time) or at-export (the continuity export stays the single redaction point). The architecture's standing law names the export the control point; the finding observes secret-shaped query values persisting at rest in the workspace's evidence files. TL3 (the evidence-contract owner) with TL2 (the journal consumers' lane) decides and records it here, BEFORE the worker implementation dispatch:

- **DL-80 ADOPT the two-layer redaction law:** (1) AT-RECORD secret-shaped query-param VALUE redaction at the browser partition's persistence boundaries — journal tab URLs, `navigated` records (requested/committed URLs) and popup-gate records carry the URL with its STRUCTURE and param NAMES preserved but secret-shaped VALUES redacted (the flauz-resources `SECRET_SHAPED_PATTERNS` detector class — the same vocabulary the vault-only law uses, applied per query-param value); (2) the continuity export REMAINS the authoritative full-redaction boundary, unchanged (belt-and-braces: the export catches non-query secret shapes the at-record layer does not target).

Architectural basis (why two layers, not one):
1. **At-rest is the exposure the finding measured:** the canary audit proved the WIRE is clean (deny path sends zero commands) while the DISK carried the secret verbatim. A control point that only redacts on export leaves raw secret values in every workspace's `.flauz/browser-sessions.jsonl` — the fix must land where the exposure is.
2. **Provenance fidelity bounds the at-record layer:** URLs are evidence — the path, the param names and the shape of the request survive redaction intact, so audit value is preserved; only secret-shaped VALUES (detected by the standing pattern vocabulary, not a blunt query-strip) are replaced. A blunt at-record query-strip would destroy provenance and is expressly NOT adopted.
3. **The export stays the authority:** the SECRET-REDACTION law's semantics, ordering and tests are untouched — the at-record layer is a narrower, earlier filter for one shape class (query-param values), not a second authority. Non-query secret shapes remain the export's exclusive beat.

Scope guards (what DL-80 does NOT change): verdict notes keep their structure (secret-shaped fragments inside them are already covered by the resources-lane detector where they surface); the continuity export's redaction law and tests untouched; TL2's journalBridge consumers read structure, not query values (compatibility verified by the bridge probe — no consumer relies on raw query values); navigation RUNTIME semantics unchanged (the browser navigates the REAL URL; only the RECORD is redacted).

Implementation routing: the P2-FIX-107 worker order pins this law; the worker implements on branch `flauz-p2fix/p2-fix-107` at the claim record's pinned base; TL4 independent retest closes the finding after landing.

#### TL1 claim record — P2-FIX-108 + P2-FIX-112 (2026-10-01)
Pinned base: `fc47f41ed195535d5801f2f382b88103e9012716` (current main at claim time — post wave-closeout `#81`; the findings were authored by TL3's readiness-audit wave at base `c27de198` and routed by TL4's routing record above). One dedicated branch per finding, cut from the pinned base only:

- **P2-FIX-108** (fixtures packaging — the flauz-environments fixed harness ships with the packaged tree) — TL1 packaging/bundler lane — branch `flauz-p2fix/p2-fix-108`
- **P2-FIX-112** (no `vscode.workspace.fs` append primitive — the platform-surface review) — TL1 platform-surface review lane — deliverable is the review record itself (station-authored control-plane text, the DL-78 decision-record precedent), landed in this same merge wave below

Per-finding acceptance contracts:
- **108** — the finding's own acceptance test: the packaging-parity job FAILS when `fixtures/` is absent from the packaged tree. Implementation: `extensions/flauz-environments/package.json` declares the packaging contract (`flauzPackagedAssets: ["fixtures"]` — the contract travels with the extension that needs the assets); `bundle-extensions.mjs` (the TL1 packaging step) stamps each declared asset alongside `dist/` (idempotent in the dev layout — the honest form of "ships fixtures next to dist/" when the packaging output is the in-place extension tree) and `--verify` asserts every declared asset present (exit 1 when absent); `packaging-parity.mjs` gains rule PP6 (packaged-asset presence, manifest-derived — every flauz-* extension declaring `flauzPackagedAssets` must carry each declared asset in the live tree; absent → FAIL), with a `packaged-asset-missing` drift fixture under `test/fixtures/packaging-parity/drifted/` wired into `verify-fixtures.sh` (expect exit 1) and a content-level case in `packaging-parity.test.mjs`. The flauz-environments README's known-residual line (the TL1 bundler note) gains its shipped annotation. Evidence level `local-real` (gate receipts on the real tree + the fixture matrix).
- **112** — the platform-surface review, decided on the API survey: the vendored `vscode.d.ts` `FileSystem` interface (extensions/flauz-resources/vscode-dts/vscode.d.ts:9777-9880) exposes exactly stat/readDirectory/createDirectory/readFile/writeFile/delete/rename/copy — NO append primitive. The sanctioned posture: read + tmp + atomic rename IS the sanctioned append strategy for vscode fs-port surfaces (already landed by TL3's partition C, PR #74 TL3C-12 — byte-preserving, non-tearing); the cost model is one read-modify-write amplification per append, recorded as the port's cost, not a defect. A platform-level append API would be a vscode API change (out of Flauz's reach without forking core — the zero-fork-critical law); no architecture change is made. #### TL-A decision record — DL-81 (P2-FIX-109, decision-first per the claim record) (2026-10-01)

P2-FIX-109's finding records that executor ops have no cancellation ports: with the landed D1 `OP_IN_FLIGHT` guard, a second op is rejected but the in-flight op itself always runs to completion — a "destroy aborts a slow start mid-effect" semantic is impossible without a cancellation port on `EnvironmentExecutor`. No standing law settles the port shape or the partial-effect reconciliation semantics (the survey of the decision log through DL-80 confirms). TL-A (the environments productionization owner, absorbing the TL3 lane) decides and records it here, BEFORE any implementation dispatch:

- **DL-81 ADOPT the cooperative cancellation port + destroy-supersede law:** (1) **The port:** `ExecutorOpContext` gains a cancellation port minted by the manager per accepted attempt — cooperative and checkpoint-observed, never a preemptive kill. The executor consults it at its effect checkpoints (pre-spawn, post-spawn/pre-confirm, between provider-retry rounds) and returns a typed CANCELLED effect result instead of continuing. The manager exposes `cancel(id, actor)` — typed `OP_NOT_IN_FLIGHT` when nothing is in flight; provenance is mandatory on the cancel exactly as on every accepted attempt. (2) **The destroy-supersede rule:** `destroy` issued while a NON-terminal op is in flight on the same environment is not `OP_IN_FLIGHT`-rejected — it cancels the in-flight op (awaits its CANCELLED completion and defensive reap) and then proceeds as the terminal op. Every other op pair keeps the D1 interleaving law unchanged (reject, never interleave). Rationale: terminal decisiveness — a destroy must never wait out a wedged start; serialization stays for every non-terminal pair. (3) **The typed outcome:** the ledger record's `result` vocabulary gains `cancelled` (distinct from `error`); the two-armed outcome envelope is unchanged — a cancelled op returns `ok: false` with a typed `OP_CANCELLED` error carrying the partial-effect facts.
- **Partial-effect reconciliation (the acceptance shape):** (a) the executor's cancellation checkpoint performs the defensive reap BEFORE returning CANCELLED (the destroy "reaps defensively" precedent); a child spawned inside the checkpoint miss-window is covered by the superseding destroy's defensive reap — no orphan child is the acceptance invariant; (b) the envelope reconciles through the EXISTING transient-phase law (crash mid-start reconciles as `stale`) — a cancelled mid-effect start lands the same reconciliation, never a silent healthy state; (c) the ledger records BOTH attempts honestly in sequence: the cancelled attempt (`result: 'cancelled'`, partial-effect facts, the cancelling actor's provenance) then the triggering op's own record (destroy ok → terminal `destroyed` envelope).
- **Scope guards:** the state machine is NOT redesigned (transition table, transient phases, terminal states hold); cancellation never fabricates completion (a cancelled start records cancelled, not started); the provider-retry window (TL2-F2B) never retries a cancelled op; simulated executors model the port deterministically; the trust-posture fail-closed gates are unaffected; the probe/describe vocabulary is untouched (P2-FIX-110's `UNVERIFIABLE` addition is a separate landing).

Implementation routing: the P2-FIX-109 worker order pins this law (§7 station note); the worker implements on branch `flauz-p2fix/p2-fix-109` at the claim record's pinned base; the finding's acceptance test (a destroy issued while a slow start is mid-effect lands a cancelled start and a terminal `destroyed` envelope, ledger recording both honestly, no orphan child) is the landing gate.

#### TL-A decision record — DL-82 (P2-FIX-111, decision-first per the claim record) (2026-10-01)

P2-FIX-111's finding records the vocabulary asymmetry: `EDGE_LEGALITY`'s `RESTORABLE_KINDS` (flauz-resources/src/api.ts) admits `task`, `agent-session` and `workflow` as `restored-from`/`snapshot-of` from-kinds, but `ContinuityService`'s `FAMILY_BY_KIND` has no v0 restoration family for them — restorable in the graph grammar, unexecutable in the service. The routing law reserves family-minting to the owning lanes (flauz-workspace task envelope; Agent OS agent sessions). TL-A decides the convergence posture here, BEFORE any implementation dispatch:

- **DL-82 ADOPT the restrict-the-matrix convergence:** `RESTORABLE_KINDS` converges to exactly the kinds with v0 restoration families — `file`, `directory`, `artifact`, `evidence`, `browser-session`, `environment`. `task`, `agent-session` and `workflow` leave the restorable from-kinds: the graph grammar stops admitting edges the service cannot execute. The typed restore error's closed-set message updates to the honest shrunk set. The matrix + tests + fixtures land as ONE DL-R2-shaped change within flauz-resources.
- **Rationale:** restriction is the only convergence that invents no second authority — minting placeholder families would duplicate the owning lanes' contracts prematurely (the routing record's exact warning), while leaving the wide matrix keeps the expressible-but-unexecutable asymmetry the finding measured. The owning lanes re-admit their kinds in their own waves when they mint their restoration families — the joint DL-R2 update (matrix + family + tests + fixtures together).
- **Scope guards:** the `derived-from` wildcard rows, the `depends-on`/`produced`/`bound-to` legality rows and every non-restoration surface are untouched; existing graphs that never used restoration edges on the three kinds are unaffected (the worker surveys consumers read-only and reports any cross-extension dependency as a boundary stop, not a cross).

Implementation routing: the P2-FIX-111 worker order pins this law; the worker implements on branch `flauz-p2fix/p2-fix-111` at the claim record's pinned base; the acceptance gate: a `restored-from`/`snapshot-of` from-kind outside the converged set is rejected by the legality check (failing tests on base, passing on fix), and a `restored-from` edge on a `file`-kind ref still resolves to the real `file-artifact` family.

#### TL-A decision record — DL-83 (P2-FIX-113, the keyed/signed-chain bounded deferral) (2026-10-01)

P2-FIX-113's finding records the unkeyed-tail residual: the landed per-row `prev` chain detects every realistic lazy-tamper class, but an attacker who rewrites an entire tail consistently (recomputing every `prev` link and the digests) together with the envelope is beyond unkeyed tamper-evidence; the final record's metadata and a restore Phase-3 hard-crash window share the class. The TL-A handoff sanctions either completing the keyed/signed-chain decision as implementation or recording an explicit bounded deferral. TL-A decides:

- **DL-83 RECORD the bounded deferral:** the unkeyed per-row chain stays the v0 tamper-evidence posture for beta — it covers single-record, reorder, delete, truncate, digest-edit, seq-edit and content-edit classes (the tamper-evidence battery enumerates them; the residual is construction-level and documented in the ledger headers, never claimed fixed). The **signed-checkpoint hook** (the DL-20 pattern — periodic keyed checkpoints anchoring the chain tail; a consistently-rewritten tail without the key fails verification at the first checkpoint boundary) is ADOPTED when its key-holding boundary is decided. **The explicit trigger:** the beta/production security gate that requires evidence-ledger tamper-evidence against a whole-tail adversary. The key-holding boundary decision (OS keychain vs user-enrolled key vs hardware root; the requirement that the key live OUT-OF-BAND relative to the ledger files — an attacker with workspace-write access must not hold the verification key) is that wave's architecture decision.
- **Deferral soundness:** unkeyed verification shares fate with the data only when an attacker controls the workspace AND the verification path together; the realistic lazy-tamper class is fully covered today; the residual requires a consistent whole-tail rewrite. Closing P2-FIX-113 by this record is the handoff's sanctioned bounded deferral — the finding's deliverable IS the decision (the P2-FIX-112 review-record precedent).

No implementation dispatches for P2-FIX-113; the finding closes with this record (landing note below).

The bounded deferral carries the explicit trigger (the beta/production security gate requiring whole-tail-adversary tamper-evidence), the adoption sketch (the signed-checkpoint hook per the DL-20 pattern) and the key-holding boundary requirement (out-of-band relative to the ledger files). The residual classes stay documented in the provenance ledger headers and the tamper-evidence battery — never claimed fixed by this closure.

#### TL-A decision record — DL-84 (P2-FIX-114, decision-first per the claim record) (2026-10-01)

P2-FIX-114's finding records the fork-critical regression on main: PR #101 (TL-B's LAB-001) landed 525 lines under `src/vs/platform/lab/**` — outside every sanctioned placement family (the additive paths first; the F-01 escape hatch is `src/vs/workbench/contrib/flauz` only, and even it is DECISION-LOG-gated). The compat-battery fork-critical row and the verify-fixtures matrix are RED on `a5a454bd5e` (1886/1887 + 1 deviation); both were GREEN on the pre-LAB-001 main `680fb00bc8e`. TL-A (the resident watch that surfaced the regression through the A-PROD-002 battery re-run, and the landing-gate owner) decides the remedy here, BEFORE any implementation dispatch:

- **DL-84 ADOPT the restrict-to-additive demotion:** `git mv src/vs/platform/lab build/flauz/lab` — a VERBATIM relocation of both files (contracts + test), ZERO content edits, on a dedicated branch cut from current main. The build/flauz/ tree is the guard-banner-sanctioned additive path for exactly this class (Flauz-owned, not budget-bearing, no upstream merge surface). The module's own test import (`../../common/labContracts.js`) is depth-identical after the move and survives UNCHANGED (verified station-side); no consumer, CI wiring or packaging surface references the old path (the read-only survey in the finding doc).
- **Rationale:** demotion restores the fork-critical law with the smallest possible change (a pure move); adjudicating an F-01-style carve-out for `src/vs/platform/lab` would instead grow the sanctioned fork surface for every future Lab wave — the exact upstream-merge burden DL-12/DL-10 exist to prevent.
- **The named demotion alternative (the law's requirement):** `extensions/flauz-lab` (a new Flauz extension). REJECTED this wave: a contracts-only, zero-dependency module does not justify churning the packaging surface (the 9-extension/36-row packaging-parity machinery, product.flauz.json rows, activation lint) — that would turn a placement correction into a packaging wave. **The explicit revisit trigger:** LAB-009 (the Lab product-console wave) — when the Lab grows a real product surface, that wave makes the extension-placement decision with its own decision record.
- **Acceptance gate:** on the fix branch AND on the merged main — `fork-critical-guard.sh --base origin/upstream/main` exits 0 with EMPTY divergence; `compat-battery --require` 1887/1887; `verify-fixtures` 0 deviations; the 9-test suite 9/9 (the isolated mocha+tsx runner, never a repo install); scoped `tsc --noEmit` exit 0.
- **Ownership honesty:** the violating landing is TL-B's (their lane, their worker); the finding, this adjudication and the demotion are TL-A's (the resident watch + landing-gate owner). TL-B's in-flight waves (LAB-002/LAB-004) rebase onto the demotion mechanically — the import path change (`src/vs/platform/lab` -> `build/flauz/lab`) is the only rebase consequence; recorded here as the cross-TL coordination note (the versioned-contract law unchanged — the contracts' content and version are untouched).

Implementation routing: the P2-FIX-114 worker order pins this law; the worker implements on branch `flauz-p2fix/p2-fix-114` at the claim-time pinned base `a5a454bd5e`; the station independently re-verifies every receipt before landing.

#### TL-B decision record — DL-85 (LAB-009 extension placement, decision-first per the DL-84 revisit trigger) (2026-10-01)

DL-84's explicit revisit trigger fires: LAB-009 (the Lab product-console wave) grows the Lab's real product surface, and this wave owns the extension-placement decision. TL-B adjudicates here, BEFORE the W1 implementation lands (the worker order is already dispatched and pins this law):

- **DL-85 ADOPT `extensions/flauz-lab` (the 10th flauz-* extension) as the Lab console's product surface:** a tree view (`flauz.lab` in the existing `flauz` view container) + commands (runNow / saveRecommendation / focusView / refreshView) with an activation-lint-clean manifest (onView/onCommand whitelist only, NO onStartupFinished, no star), auto-discovered by `bundle-extensions.mjs` (glob, never hardcoded), and packaging-parity rows for the new surface. The extension family is the fork's sanctioned product lane — the flauz-agent..flauz-workflow precedent; compat-battery treats `extensions/flauz-*` as fork-side by prefix (zero upstream merge surface).
- **The deterministic core STAYS at `build/flauz/lab/` (single source of truth):** the 5 landed modules and their station-gate lane (mocha/tdd over the build lane) are untouched by this decision. The extension consumes the core through TYPE-ONLY contract imports (erased at runtime under `verbatimModuleSyntax` — the node --test lane never resolves them) plus an injected `LabConsolePort` seam; the runtime binding (`labFlauzPort.ts`, runtime `.js` specifiers resolved by esbuild at bundle time) is the ONLY cross-lane runtime edge, and no test ever imports it (Node type stripping cannot resolve the lab core's `.js` specifiers — verified station-side by direct probe before the packet was frozen).
- **Rationale:** the DL-84 cost argument ("a contracts-only, zero-dependency module does not justify churning the packaging surface") inverts at exactly this wave — the surface IS the deliverable, so the parity rows are the honest record, not churn. Splitting placement this way keeps each lane at its cheapest honest home: the core stays where its landing records and gate receipts live; the console lands where the product machinery (bundle/activation/parity) already governs.
- **REJECTED: relocating the core into the extension** (`extensions/flauz-lab/src/lab/**`): churns 5 landing records, the mocha test lane and the parity citations for zero product gain — the core is lane-stable and station-gated where it is; the console is the moving surface.
- **REJECTED: a webview-based console:** webviews add an iframe/asset surface and their own parity questions; the tree-view + commands console covers the W1 capability list (run / select scenario / inspect candidates / compare / save) with the platform's native surfaces.
- **The closed apply seam (law, user-visible):** "apply a recommendation through normal Flauz execution" stays REFUSED in W1 — the refusal reason is shown to the user on every save; applying routes through Agent OS via the LAB-010 bridge, and "the Lab never becomes a second execution engine" (the MASTER-ROADMAP LAB-010 law, restated as the extension's own honest closed door).
- **Acceptance gates (on the W1 branch AND merged main):** activation-lint exit 0 (the 10th flauz-* manifest, discovered by glob); packaging-parity green with the 2 new rows (node-free citations) and ZERO DRIFT on existing rows; the compat-battery fork-critical row unaffected (zero src/vs touches by construction); the extension's `node --test` suite green; scoped `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `bundle-extensions --verify` green post-merge (the dist tree ships).
- **Ownership honesty:** TL-B's lane, TL-B's decision — the DL-84 revisit trigger handed this adjudication to the LAB-009 wave explicitly. The W1 implementation routes through the replay worker lane (the station implements nothing; decision records and registry text are the station's control-plane deliverables per the DL-78/112 precedent).

#### TL-A claim record + decision record DL-86 — A-PROD-006-W1 (the incident/problem registry plane) (2026-10-04)

**Claim:** TL-A claims A-PROD-006 (production launch and post-production operations), opening with W1 — the incident/problem registry plane, the product machinery of the handoff's A6 law (every material production issue follows incident → reproducible finding → registry item → fix → regression → release → post-release verification). The launch + post-release-acceptance plane (W2) queues behind W1; the A-PROD-003 deeper dogfood waves (the agent-with-tools lane) run as the parallel production-usage feed, their surfaces disjoint from this wave's (extensions/flauz-incidents/** vs build/flauz/dogfood/**).

**DL-86 — the closed-loop authority law (decided BEFORE the W1 dispatch):**

- **DL-86 ADOPT the two-registry separation:** the workspace-local incident ledger (`.flauz/incidents/`, record kind `flauz.incidents/v1`, the OPERATIONAL loop state — census-visible, append-only, swept fail-closed for secret-shaped values per the family's privacy law) and the repository control plane (`WORK-REGISTRY.md` — the AUTHORITY for work items) are separate authorities. The product surface RECORDS the loop state and LINKS to control-plane state by carrying the repo-side registry item id VERBATIM at the registry-item stage; it never mints, edits, ranks or supersedes control-plane state (no second persistence authority — the ARCHITECTURE-LOCK's §11 law applied to the incident lane).
- **The loop-stage vocabulary is frozen verbatim from the handoff A6:** `incident → reproducible-finding → registry-item → fix → regression → release → post-release-verification`. Typed forward transitions only, each transition evidence-bearing; the ONE reopen law: a failed post-release verification reopens the incident with the failure as evidence (a closed loop never hides a regression).
- **The closure law (fail-closed):** the `fix → regression` transition REQUIRES regression evidence (the named regression test's passing receipt); `regression → release` requires the release identity (the owning release/checklist record pointer); the loop closes ONLY on post-release verification evidence — a re-run of the named owning checks against the released state, banked as the closing receipt. Missing evidence = typed REFUSAL with the exact missing stage-evidence kind, never a silent skip; no stage may be skipped forward.
- **The source-binding law:** an incident originates from a TYPED source — the telemetry failure-census vocabulary (`flauz.failures.list`), the durability escalation-demand records, the dogfood friction log (`flauz.dogfood-friction/v1`) — or is typed `manual` (operator-entered, disclosed as such). Never a fabricated or inferred incident; a source binding that cannot resolve is a typed disclosure, not a dropped row.
- **The evidence-label law:** every stage transition carries its evidence level from the frozen ladder (`fixture | simulated | local-real | runtime-real | live-provider | production-real`); the loop never promotes a label by wording — the same law as the program's evidence law, applied per transition.
- **Placement:** `extensions/flauz-incidents` (the 20th flauz-* extension) — the sanctioned product lane per the DL-85 precedent (extensions/flauz-* prefix = fork-side, zero upstream merge surface), command-only activation (onCommand/onView whitelist only, no onStartupFinished, no star), auto-discovered by bundle-extensions.mjs, packaging-parity rows additive at landing (65 → 68 rows expected), SBOM component row additive.
- **Scope guards (what DL-86 does NOT change):** the telemetry/durability/dogfood source surfaces are READ as sources only (zero edits to those extensions this wave); the production gate's eleven prove-items stay verbatim (the incident plane is post-production OPERATIONS, not a new prove-item — the launch plane (W2) binds it, with its own decision record if the gate vocabulary must grow); WORK-REGISTRY.md control-plane edits remain TL-owned station acts.

Implementation routing: the A-PROD-006-W1 worker order pins this law (§7); the worker implements on branch `flauz-aprod/aprod-006-w1-incidents` at the pinned base `07f1decc` (the DL-86 decision head); the station independently re-verifies every receipt before landing (the ten-gate table) and updates this registry in the same merge wave.

### Independence / ownership law

- TL-A does not depend on the Engineering Lab for production readiness.
- TL-B does not depend on TL-A implementation for simulation/search milestones.
- Cross-TL work uses versioned contracts, fixtures or mock adapters.
- No worker branch is cherry-picked into another TL branch.
- Shared control-plane updates are made only by the registered owner of the control-plane wave.
- Architecture changes require a decision record before implementation.
- DONE requires implementation on `main`, targeted evidence and registry update in the same merge wave.

### Completion target

The program is complete only when:

1. TL-A closes the inherited gap tail and proves production operation.
2. TL-B proves the Engineering Lab closed loop.
3. The shared LabRecommendation -> AgentTask/Workflow integration is independently re-verified.
4. Dogfood, beta and production gates are complete.
5. Post-production reliability and calibration loops are operational.

### Protected TL-A surfaces

#### TL-A decision record DL-87 — A-PROD-006-W2 (the launch + post-release-acceptance plane) (2026-10-05, decided BEFORE the W2 dispatch)

**Claim:** W2 is the launch plane proper — the machinery that makes the incidents loop's last two stages REAL: the `regression -> release` transition's `release-identity` evidence and the `release -> post-release-verification` closure's `post-release-verification-receipt` evidence. W1 built the loop; W2 builds what the loop's evidence kinds point AT.

- **DL-87 ADOPT the acceptance-record plane:** a NEW 21st extension `extensions/flauz-acceptance` (surfaces disjoint from both siblings — zero edits to flauz-release/** and flauz-incidents/**; both are READ through contract-pinned parsers). Operational state at `.flauz/acceptance/` (record kind `flauz.acceptance/v1`); the two-registry separation applies (WORK-REGISTRY.md stays the control plane; acceptance records never mint registry items).
- **The launch-act law (fail-closed):** `flauz.acceptance.launch` mints a release-acceptance record ONLY over a GREEN, FRESH `flauz.release.checklist` artifact (reads `.flauz/release/checklist-<stamp>.json` through the contract-pinned parser; refuses typed on red/absent/torn). The record carries: the acceptance id `flauz:acc:<16-hex>`, the bound checklist artifact path VERBATIM (the release identity the incidents loop carries), the pinned product state (the version-inventory + census snapshot shapes, contract-pinned from flauz-release), and the NAMED OWNING CHECKS set (the release's acceptance set — the checklist row kinds + the incident's named regression test when launched to close an incident loop).
- **The post-release-acceptance law (the closing receipt):** `flauz.acceptance.verify` re-runs the named owning checks against the released state and banks the closing receipt — pass or FAIL, both typed, both banked. A FAILED receipt is the typed failure record the incidents reopen law consumes (the closed loop never hides a regression). The receipt's evidence label comes from the frozen ladder and is never promoted by wording; a check the acceptance plane cannot itself evaluate is a TYPED DISCLOSURE row naming its owning surface (the honest-scope law), never a silent green.
- **The gate vocabulary does NOT grow (DL-86's scope guard honored):** the eleven prove-items stay verbatim; the launch plane binds the gate by READING its verdict from the checklist artifacts, never by redefining it.
- **Placement:** `extensions/flauz-acceptance` — command-only activation, auto-discovered by bundle-extensions.mjs, packaging-parity rows additive at landing (68 -> 71), SBOM component row additive (20 -> 21). Implementation on branch `flauz-aprod/aprod-006-w2-acceptance` at the pinned base `7bb05feb` (the post-repair green head); the station re-verifies every receipt (ten-gate table) — as station-EXECUTED re-runs, the G10 lesson of the W1 repair record.

- **Station review (all gates exact at `018bc5f4`):** G1-G2 tsc CLEAN · G3 mocha 64/64 (report 16 / advance 15 / status 16 / contract 17 — the station's own per-suite execution) · G4 determinism grep CLEAN · G5/G6 additive-only (parity 65->68 = 93+0; SBOM 19->20 additive) · G7 placement clean (only the 3 allowed paths). The station reconstruction replayed the worker's 51 file ops in TRUE block order (the manifest sort bug root-caused and fixed — pure (msg_ts, block, call) sort); the 6 ghost-edit residuals hand-resolved tab-faithfully; the worker's own bash heredocs recovered verbatim from the batch store.
- **The station integration (`3fbd280f`):** the flauz-production CAPABILITY_CATALOG grows the incident-registry-loop entry (production 66/66); the flauz-isolation law table grows the incidentsRecords row + KNOWN_EXTENSIONS knows flauz-incidents (test pins: surfaces 30->31, workspaceBound 26->27, the prefix set +loop- — isolation 69/69); `security-runtime-gate --generate` minted the manifest at the integration head: **20 extensions / 20 artifacts, double-bundle byte-identical** (17/20 pins byte-identical; the ONE new pin flauz-incidents; the 2 lawfully source-changed pins flauz-isolation + flauz-production — the station's own integration edits); the SBOM regenerated canonically (the worker's hand-added row carried an indent glitch; instrument-form now, gate row PASS); packaging-parity CLEAN (20 extensions, 68 rows, 0 drift); activation-lint GREEN; verify-fixtures 222 ok + 3 PRE-EXISTING real-tree deviations (identical at the pre-integration base `c28f46ce` — environmental, honestly disclosed).

The cure — a station-side transparent spacing relay (the drill untouched, the repo untouched): BASE_URL pointed at a local pass-through that (a) holds each request 15ms so the retry row's 1ms-aborted client connections are discarded BEFORE the upstream (zero QPS cost — the abort is client-local), and (b) delays each response 550ms so successive rows' upstream requests never share a QPS window. Every request and response passes through verbatim (headers + body + SSE streaming); the endpoint, the credentials, the auth failures and the completions are the real gateway's own.

#### Station landing record — the C-ENV CI-flake family ROOT-CAUSE REPAIR (the bootstrap-race cure) (2026-10-09)

- **The defect class (three consecutive PR sightings, two modes, both self-healing on re-run):** PR #171 (run 37914224270) the BOOT-TIMEOUT mode — `boot.driver-report FAIL: the test-driver extension did not run within 180s` + `flauz-environments never activated` (orthogonality proven first: the CR-009 delta never touched the drill's surfaces); PR #172 and PR #176 the BOOTSTRAP-RACE mode — `registry inactive (no workspace folder or failed bootstrap)`, the resolver rows PASSing except the two that raced the workbench's workspace-folder bootstrap. Root cause (one sentence): activate() fires the workspace bootstrap DETACHED, so a registry-dependent command or authority resolution arriving between activation and the bootstrap's completion failed closed with a message that misreported a boot-in-flight as a missing/failed boot — and the drill had no readiness wait, while the evaluator's 180s report budget was too tight for a loaded public runner's whole-boot tail.
- **The repair (three coordinated surfaces, one root cause):** (1) the PRODUCT gate — `extensions/flauz-environments/src/bootGate.ts` (the pure activation-bootstrap gate: `run`/`settled`/`isRunning`; a FAILED bootstrap settles its waiters, never fabricates readiness, never rejects the run promise; a retry REPLACES the tracked promise) wired into `src/extension.ts` (activate's detached boot + retryBootstrap route through `state.boot.run`; EVERY registry/lifecycle/continuity-dependent command handler and `resolveEnvironment` await `awaitBoot()` first — a command racing the boot now WAITS for it, then re-reads its own state and keeps the exact fail-closed behavior when nothing booted or the boot failed); (2) the DRILL guard row — `test/fixtures/resolver-driver/main.js` gains `driver.registry-ready` (polls `flauz.env.list` within a 240s readiness budget, retrying ONLY the inactive-bootstrap rejection; a non-bootstrap error or a budget expiry FAILS the row honestly and short-circuits the dependent rows with the reason — the evaluator's row iteration is generic, no evaluator change needed); (3) the CI budget — `flauz-environments.yml` rung-2 report-timeout 180s -> 360s (the #171 mode's medicine: the job's own 60m timeout dwarfs it; the driver's internal 60s extension-wait + 240s readiness budgets fit with headroom).
- **Station receipts (all executed at the repair tree):** extension unit suite 279 tests / 275 pass / 0 fail / 4 documented live-drill skips (the +6 = the new `test/bootGate.test.ts`: waiter-not-early, failed-boot-settles, sync-throw-survival, retry-slot-replacement, captured-promise independence) · scoped `tsc --noEmit` EXIT 0 under the repo-pinned `@typescript/typescript6` compiler · activation-lint GREEN (21 manifests, the R3 onStartupFinished cap untouched — the gate is wiring-internal, no activation-event change) · C-ENV fixture canary GREEN (A1-A8) · verify-fixtures ALL 232 CASES AS EXPECTED (0 deviations — no fixture pins the driver fixture's bytes) · premium-ux-gate CLEAN (7 views / 103 titles / 17 copies) · ia-gate CLEAN · packaging-parity CLEAN (21 extensions / 71 rows / 0 drift) · fork-critical guard PASS (ledger EMPTY) · the evaluator exercised END-TO-END against a synthetic driver report: verdict PASS 12/12 rows including `driver.registry-ready`, and the FAIL-direction proven (a registry-ready FAIL row exits 1 — never a fake pass) · `node --check` on the driver fixture · the workflow YAML structurally validated (rung2 job, 10 steps, the 360000 flag wired).
- **Behavioral disclosures (honest):** `flauz.env.list` and `flauz.env.showPlan` handlers are now async (their executeCommand return is a promise — the ONLY architecture-allowed invocation surface is `vscode.commands.executeCommand`, which awaits; no synchronous consumer exists); the ENVIRONMENT_ABSENT fail-closed law is UNCHANGED in its settled semantics (no workspace folder -> immediate typed failure; failed bootstrap -> immediate typed failure; the only new behavior is the bounded WAIT while a bootstrap is genuinely in flight — the resolution path's `resolveAttempt` re-check discipline untouched); the drill's row set grew by one guard row (the CI's honest-observation surface, not a product surface).
- **The standing C-ENV flake item is CLOSED by this repair** (the root-cause routing from the W6 record's standing list); the live-provider row and the host swap remain environment-blocked standing items (no credentials / no root in this sandbox — unchanged).
- **THE ROADMAP-COMPLETE DECLARATION (the resident watch's monitor -> harvest -> review -> approve -> dispatch-next loop, closed):** the Phase C-R roadmap is COMPLETE in its terminal form — every item landed and recorded (CR-001/002 wave 1 · CR-003/008/012 wave 3 · CR-005 wave 4 · CR-004 wave 5 · CR-009 wave 6 + the compounds supplement · CR-013 wave 7: the terminal acceptance PR #175 + the runner instrument PR #174); the map carries FIVE wired entries (ZC-003/ZC-004/ZC-005/ZC-008/ZC-009), the terminal ledger reads closed=5 / exercised-still-gap=3 / unexercised-still-gap=2 pinned by BOTH the simulation suite and the acceptance runner; the formal acceptance record is the program's closing evidence artifact; this release v0.1.3 is the release of the completed program. **Nothing promoted, nothing faked.**
